United States\n|attendance= \n|gate= \n|previousevent=[[World Series of Fighting 35: Ivanov vs. Jordan|WSOF35: Ivanov vs. Jordan]]\n|followingevent=[[PFL Everett]]\n}}\n'''PFL Fight Night: Daytona''', also known as '''PFL Daytona''', was a [[mixed martial arts]] event promoted by the [[Professional Fighters League]] that was held on June 30, 2017 at the [[Daytona International Speedway]] in [[Daytona Beach, Florida]] and aired live on the [[NBC Sports Network]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2017/06/pfl-daytona-results-jon-fitch-survives-early-trouble-submits-brian-foster|title='PFL: Daytona' results: Jon Fitch survives early trouble, submits Brian Foster|date=July 1, 2017}} \n\nIt was the first event to be promoted under the PFL name following the rebranding of World Series Of Fighting by its new owners, MMAX Investment Partners.\n\n==Background==\nThe event took place in the infield at the Daytona International Speedway as a planned precursor to the [[Coca-Cola Firecracker 250]] NASCAR XFINITY Series race, but the race was postponed until July 1 due to rain, and the fights began several hours earlier than expected. \n\nThe show drew an average of 291,000 viewers on [[NBC Sports Network]] and peaked at 495,000 for the headliner, giving the network its second-largest audience ever viewership at that time.{{Cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Professional-Fighters-League-Inaugural-Event-Peaks-at-495000-Viewers-on-NBCSN-124405|title=Professional Fighters League Inaugural Event Peaks at 495,000 Viewers on NBCSN|website=Sherdog}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Jon Fitch]] \n|def.\n|[[Brian Foster (fighter)|Brian vacated on June 7, 2018, when the PFL league format began at [[PFL 1 (2018 season)|PFL 1]]. Tyler Vogel replaced Santos against Taylor. event\n|name=World Series of Fighting 15: Branch vs. Okami\n|image=\n|promotion=[[World Series of Fighting]]\n|date= November 15, 2014\n|venue=[[USF Sun Dome]]\n|city= [[Tampa, Florida|Tampa]], Florida, United States\n|attendance=7,000\n|gate=\n|previousevent=[[World Series of Fighting 14: Ford vs. Shields]]\n|followingevent=[[World Series of Fighting 16: Palhares vs. Fitch]]\n}}\n\n'''World Series of Fighting 15: Branch vs. Okami''' was a mixed martial arts event held November 15, 2014 in [[Tampa, Florida|Tampa]], Florida, United States. The event aired on [[NBCSN]] in the US and on [[TSN2]] in Canada.\n\n==Background==\nThe main event was a fight for the WSOF Middleweight Championship between champion [[David Branch (fighter)|David Branch]] and challenger [[Yushin Okami]].{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/WSOF-15-to-be-Held-at-University-of-South-Floridas-Sun-Dome-in-Tampa-75033|title=WSOF 15 to be Held at University of South Florida's Sun Dome in Tampa|publisher=sherdog.com}}\n\nThe co-main{{Clarify|date=July 2025}} event between [[Justin Gaethje (fighter)|Justin Gaethje]] and [[Melvin Guillard]] was originally scheduled to be for the [[List of WSOF champions#Lightweight Championship|WSOF Lightweight Championship]]. However, Guillard missed the 155 pound weight allowance for the title fight, weighing 158.8 pounds. As a result, the bout was changed to a non-title catchweight bout with Guillard agreeing to give fifty percent of his purse to Gaethje. In the main event on the preliminary card Maurice Salmon was able to score a [[TKO]] victory via doctor stoppage over Javier Torres. Early in the bout Torres complained to the referee about eye pokes, but the bout continued and the two kept the action going. Salmon was able to land a devastating left and a flurry of ground and pound until the bell. After an eye injury the bout stopped in the first round. Salmon told reporters he was displeased with Torres head butting during the weigh-ins.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/11/wsof-15-weigh-in-results-guillard-misses-weight-gaethje-bout-now-non-title|title=WSOF 15 weigh-in results: Guillard misses weight, Gaethje bout now non-title fight| publisher=mmajunkie.com}}\n\nThis event aired against [[UFC 180]] and [[Bellator 131]] making it the first time three major martial arts promotion's air events on the same night.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2014/9/23/6832599/wsof-15-moved-to-nov-15-goes-head-to-head-with-ufc-180-and-bellator|title=WSOF 15 moved to Nov. 15, goes head-to-head with UFC 180 and Bellator 131|date=23 September 2014 |publisher=mmafighting.com}}\n\n==Results==\n{{-}}\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n| Middleweight\n| [[David Branch (fighter)|David Branch]] Holder's original opponent, Jake Heun, remained on the card and fought [[Clifford Starks]]. Starks' original opponent, Eddie Arizmendi, was removed from the card entirely but still received his show and win money.\n\n==Results==\n{{-}}\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card}}\n{{MMAevent This event aired on [[NBCSN]] in the U.S and on [[Fight Network]] in Canada.\n\n==Background==\nThe main event was originally scheduled to feature a middleweight fight between [[Yushin Okami]] and WSOF Canadian Welterweight Champion [[Ryan Ford (fighter)|Ryan Ford]].{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Ryan-FordYushin-Okami-Smealinho-RamaBlagoi-Ivanov-Announced-For-WSOF-21-81727|title=Ryan Ford-Yushin Okami, Smealinho Rama-Blagoi Ivanov announced for WSOF 21}} However, Ford was forced out of the bout due to an injury and the pairing was scrapped.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/04/injured-ryan-ford-says-hes-out-of-junes-wsof-21-headliner|title=Injured Ryan Ford says he's out of June's WSOF 21 headliner|publisher=mmajunkie.com |author=Staff|date=2015-04-07|access-date=2015-04-08}}\n\nThe main event was then changed to a WSOF Featherweight Championship fight between champion [[Lance Palmer]] and [[World Extreme Cagefighting|WEC]] & [[Bellator MMA]] veteran [[Chris Horodecki]].\n\nThe co-main event featured a WSOF Heavyweight Championship fight between champion Smealinho Rama and Bellator MMA veteran [[Blagoy Ivanov]].\n\n==Results==\n{{-}}\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (NBCSN)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n| Featherweight\n| [[Lance Palmer]] (c)\n| def.\n| [[Chris Horodecki]]\n| Submission (neck States]]. This event aired on [[NBCSN]] in the U.S and on [[Fight Network]] in Canada.\n\n==Background==\nThe event went head-to-head with [[UFC 190]] on the same night.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/05/wsof-22-stacks-deck-vs-ufc-190-with-championship-triple-header-new-flyweight-belt|title=WSOF 22 stacks deck vs. UFC 190 with championship triple-header, flyweight title|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=John Morgan|date=2015-05-26|access-date=2015-05-26}}\n\nThe main event was scheduled to be a fight for the WSOF Welterweight Championship between champion [[Rousimar Palhares]] making his second defence of his title against challenger [[Jake Shields]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/04/wsof-22-palhares-vs-shields-set-for-aug-1-in-las-vegas|title='WSOF 22: Palhares vs. Shields' set for Aug. 1 in Las Vegas|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=John Morgan|date=2015-04-20|access-date=2015-04-21}}\n\nThe co-main event was scheduled to be a fight for the WSOF Bantamweight Championship between champion [[Marlon Moraes]] making his second defence of his title against challenger [[Sheymon Moraes]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2015/5/13/8596967/marlon-moraes-to-defend-title-against-sheymon-moraes-in-wsof-22-co|title=Marlon Moraes to defend title against Sheymon Moraes in WSOF 22 co-main event|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Marc Raimondi|date=2015-05-13|access-date=2015-05-13}}\n\n[[Tyrone Spong]] was expected to face [[Mike Kyle]] at the event. However, Spong pulled out of the bout due to an injury and was replaced by [[Thiago Silva (fighter)|Thiago Silva]].{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Thiago-Silva-Replaces-Injured-Tyrone-Spong-Faces-Mike-Kyle-at-WSOF-22-88725|title=Thiago Silva replaces Tyrone Spong, faces Mike Kyle at WSOF 22|publisher=sherdog.com|author=Tristen Critchfield|date=2015-07-07|access-date=2015-07-07}} In turn, Silva was forced out of the bout after being deemed ineligible to receive a license by the [[Nevada Athletic Commission|Nevada State Athletic Commission]]. Short notice replacement [[Clifford Starks]] stepped in to face Kyle at the event.{{cite web|url=http://themmareport.com/2015/07/clifford-stark-replaces-thiago-silva-on-saturdays-wsof-22-against-mike-kyle/|title=Clifford Starks replaces Thiago Silva on Saturday\u2019s WSOF 22 against Mike Kyle|publisher=themmareport.com|author=Staff|date=2015-07-28|access-date=2015-07-28}}\n\n==Results==\n{{-}}\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (NBCSN)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n| Welterweight\n| punches)\n|1\n|4:30\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent end|notes=yes}}\n\n==Standings after event==\nThe point system consists of outcome based scoring and bonuses for an early win. Under the outcome based scoring system, the winner of a fight receives 3 points and the loser receives 0 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. If a fighter misses weight, then the fighter that missed weight will receive 0 points and his opponent will receive 3 points due to a walkover (WO) victory.{{cite web|url=http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|title=Rules and Regulations|publisher=PFL|access-date=2018-06-05|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180808170826/http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|archive-date=2018-08-08|url-status=dead}}\n\n===Featherweight===\n{| class=\"wikitable sortable\"\n! 2\n|promotion=[[Professional Fighters League]]\n|date= {{start date|2018|06|21}}\n|venue= [[Chicago Theatre]]\n|city= [[Chicago]], [[Illinois]]\n|attendance= \n|estimatedviewers = 150,000{{Cite web|url=http://mmapayout.com/2018/08/pfl-6-draws-138000-viewers-on-nbc-sports-network/|title=PFL 6 draws 138,000 viewers on NBC Sports Network : MMAPayout.com: The Business of MMA|website=mmapayout.com|access-date=2018-08-19}}\n|previousevent=[[PFL 1 (2018 season)|PFL 1]]\n|followingevent=[[PFL 3 (2018 season)|PFL 3]]\n}}\n{{Main|PFL (2018 season)}}\nThe '''''PFL 2''''' [[mixed martial arts]] event for the 2018 season of the [[Professional Fighters League]] was held on June 21, 2018, at the [[Chicago Theatre]] in [[Chicago]], [[Illinois]]. It was the second regular season event of 2018 and included only fights in the [[lightweight]] and [[light heavyweight]] divisions.{{cite web|url=https://www.pflmma.com/event/pfl-event-2|title=Fight Card - PFL 2|publisher=PFL|access-date=2018-08-28|archive-date=2018-08-28|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180828035806/https://www.pflmma.com/event/pfl-event-2|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Background==\n[[Rashid Magomedov]] was expected to face [[Jason High]] at this event, however Magomedev was removed from the bout due to injury and replaced by [[Efra\u00edn Escudero]].{{cite news|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2018/6/14/17464824/efrain-escudero-replaces-injured-rashid-magomedev-against-jason-high-at-pfl-2|title=Efrain Escudero replaces injured Rashid Magomedev against Jason High at PFL 2|publisher=MMAFighting|author=Marc Raimondi|date=2018-06-14|access-date=2018-06-14}} On weigh in day, the fight suffered another set back as Escudero missed the 156-pound weight limit by six pounds. As a result, Escudero became ineligible to earn points regardless of the outcome.\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (NBCSN)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Will Atajev]]\n|TKO (punches)\n|3\n|4:31\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent end|notes=yes}}\n\n==Standings after event==\nThe point system consists of outcome based scoring and bonuses for an early win. Under the outcome based scoring system, the winner of a fight receives 3 points and the loser receives 0 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. If a fighter misses weight, then the fighter that missed weight will receive 0 points and his opponent will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory.{{cite web|url=http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|title=Rules and Regulations|publisher=PFL|access-date=2018-06-07|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180808170826/http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|archive-date=2018-08-08|url-status=dead}}\n\n===Lightweight===\n{| class=\"wikitable sortable\"\n! PFL 3|publisher=PFL}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (NBCSN)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Ray Cooper III]]\n|def.\n|[[Jake Shields]] \n|TKO (punches)\n|2\n|2:09\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|Pavlo Kusch\n|def.\n|[[Abubakar Nurmagomedov]] \n|Submission (rear-naked choke)\n|2\n|1:23\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Rick Story]]\n|def.\n|[[Yuri Villefort]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (30-27, 30-27, 30-27)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Shamil Gamzatov]]\n|def.\n|[[Eddie Gordon (fighter)|Eddie Gordon]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (30-27, 29-28, 29-28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[John Howard (fighter)|John Howard]]\n|def.\n|[[Gasan Umalatov]]\n|Submission (rear-naked choke)\n|2\n|2:59\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent card|Preliminary card (Facebook Watch)|header=no}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Magomed Magomedkerimov]]\n|def.\n|[[Herman Terrado]]\n|Submission (rear-naked choke)\n|1\n|4:54\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|Jo\u00e3o Zeferino\n|def.\n|[[Paul Bradley (fighter)|Paul Bradley]]\n|TKO (punches)\n|1\n|1:58\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Bruno Santos]]\n|def.\n|[[Sadibou Sy]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (29-28, 29-28, 29-28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Louis Taylor]]\n|def.\n|Anderson Gon\u00e7alves\n|TKO (punches)\n|3\n|0:58\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|Rex Harris\n|def.\n|Andre Lobato\n|Decision (split) (29-28, 28-29, 29-28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|Abuspiyan Magomedov\n|def.\n|[[Danillo Villefort]]\n|TKO (body kick and punches)\n|1\n|3:37\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Bojan Veli\u010dkovi\u0107]]\n|def.\n|Jonatan Westin\n|TKO (punches)\n|2\n|2:50\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent end|notes=yes}}\n\n==Standings after event==\nThe point system consists of outcome based scoring and bonuses for an early win. Under the outcome based scoring system, the winner of a fight receives 3 points and the loser receives 0 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. If a fighter misses weight, then the fighter that missed weight will receive 0 points and his opponent will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory.{{cite web|url=http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|title=Rules and Regulations|publisher=PFL|access-date=2018-06-07|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180808170826/http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|archive-date=2018-08-08|url-status=dead}}\n\n===Welterweight===\n{| class=\"wikitable sortable\"\n! MMA|website=mmapayout.com|access-date=2018-08-19}}\n|previousevent=[[PFL 3 (2018 season)|PFL 3]]\n|followingevent=[[PFL 5 (2018 season)|PFL 5]]\n}}\n{{Main|PFL (2018 season)}}\nThe '''''PFL 4''''' [[mixed martial arts]] event for the 2018 season of the [[Professional Fighters League]] was held on July 19, 2018, at the [[Nassau Coliseum]] in [[Uniondale, New York]]. This was the fourth regular season event of 2018 and included fights in the [[featherweight]] and [[heavyweight]] divisions.{{cite web|url=https://www.pflmma.com/event/pfl-event-4|title=Fight Card - PFL 4|publisher=PFL}}\n\n==Background==\n\nOriginally fights were set between Bekbulat Magomedov vs. Magomed Idrisov and [[Timur Valiev]] vs. Lee Coville, however Idrisov and Coville were forced to pull out for undisclosed reasons. At first Carl Deaton and Darrick Minner were scheduled as replacements, but on weigh in day both missed the 146-pound limit, and as a result PFL elected to pull them off the card, and instead pair up their intended opponents to create Bekbulat Magomedov vs. [[Timur Valiev]].\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (NBCSN)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Featherweight\n|[[Lance Palmer]]\n|def.\n|[[Zhumabek Tursyn]]\n|Submission (rear-naked choke)\n|3\n|4:34\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Featherweight\n|Andre Harrison\n|def.\n|[[Nazareno Malegarie]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (30-27, 30-27, 30-27)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Francimar Barroso]]\n|def.\n|Jack May\n|Submission (arm triangle)\n|1\n|1:36\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Featherweight\n|[[Steven Siler]]\n|def.\n|Alexandre Almeida\n|Technical Submission (guillotine choke)\n|1\n|0:34\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Philipe Lins]]\n|def.\n|[[Alex Nicholson (fighter)|Alex Nicholson]]\n|TKO (punches)\n|2\n|3:39\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent card|Preliminary card (Facebook Watch)|header=no}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Kelvin Tiller]]\n|def.\n|[[Jared Rosholt]]\n|Submission (guillotine choke)\n|2\n|0:54\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Featherweight\n|[[Timur Valiev]]\n|def.\n|Bekbulat Magomedov\n|Decision (unanimous) (30-27, 30-27, 30-27)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Featherweight\n|Max Coga\n|def.\n|[[Marcos Galvao]]\n|TKO (punches)\n|3\n|2:19\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|Josh Copeland\n|def.\n|[[Shawn Jordan]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (29-28, 29-28, 29-28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Valdrin Istrefi]]\n|def.\n|[[Daniel Gallemore]]\n|TKO (leg kicks)\n|2\n|1:42\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent end|notes=yes}}\n\n==Standings After Event==\nThe point system consists of outcome based scoring and bonuses for an early win. Under the outcome based scoring system, the winner of a fight receives 3 points and the loser receives 0 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. If a fighter misses weight, then the fighter that missed weight will receive 0 points and his opponent will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory.{{cite web|url=http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|title=Rules and Regulations|publisher=PFL|access-date=2018-06-30|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180808170826/http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|archive-date=2018-08-08|url-status=dead}}\n\n===Featherweight===\n{| class=\"wikitable sortable\"\n! (2018 season)}}\nThe '''''PFL 5''''' [[mixed martial arts]] event for the 2018 season of the [[Professional Fighters League]] was held on August 2, 2018, at the [[Nassau Coliseum]] in [[Uniondale, New York]]. This is the fifth regular season event of 2018 and included only fights in the [[lightweight]] and [[light heavyweight]] divisions.{{cite web|url=https://www.pflmma.com/event/pfl-event-5|title=Fight Card - PFL 5|publisher=PFL}}{{Cite news|url=https://mmajunkie.com/2018/07/brian-foster-vs-natan-schulte-headlines-pfl-5-new-york-long-island|title=Brian Foster vs. Natan Schulte headlines PFL 5 in New York|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2018-07-23|access-date=2018-07-23}}\n\n==Background==\n[[Brian Foster (fighter)|Brian Foster]] was expected to face [[Natan Schulte]] at this event. However, Foster was removed due to an unspecified illness and replaced by [[Jason High]].{{cite news|url=https://mmajunkie.com/2018/07/brian-foster-out-jason-high-vs-natan-schulte-now-headlines-pfl-5-in-new-york|title=Brian Foster out, Jason High vs. Natan Schulte now headlines PFL 5 in New York|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=MMAjunkie Staff|date=2018-07-30}}\n\nOriginally a fight was set between [[Islam Mamedov]] and [[Efra\u00edn Escudero]]. However, Escudero missed the 156-pound weight limit by over seven pounds, and the bout was scratched from the card all together.{{cite news|url=https://www.newsday.com/sports/mixed-martial-arts/efrain-escudero-misses-weight-and-his-pfl-5-fight-gets-scrapped-w91492|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180802011358/https://www.newsday.com/sports/mixed-martial-arts/efrain-escudero-misses-weight-and-his-pfl-5-fight-gets-scrapped-1.20235422|url-status=live|archive-date=August 2, 2018|title=Efrain Escudero misses weight and his PFL 5 fight gets scrapped|publisher=newsday|author=Ryan Gerbosi Staff|date=2018-08-01}} As a result, Mamedov was awarded 3 points via walkover victory.\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (NBCSN)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Natan Schulte]]\n|def.\n|[[Jason High]]\n|Technical Submission (rear-naked bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Thiago Tavares]]\n|def.\n|Arthur Estr\u00e1zulas\n|Decision (split) (29-28, 28-29, 29-28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent end|notes=yes}}\n\n==Standings after event==\nThe point system consists of outcome based scoring and bonuses for an early win. Under the outcome based scoring system, the winner of a fight receives 3 points and the loser receives 0 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. If a fighter misses weight, then the fighter that missed weight will receive 0 points and his opponent will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory.{{cite web|url=http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|title=Rules and Regulations|publisher=PFL|access-date=2018-07-20|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180808170826/http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|archive-date=2018-08-08|url-status=dead}}\n\n===Lightweight===\n{| class=\"wikitable sortable\"\n! after event==\nThe point system consists of outcome based scoring and bonuses for an early win. Under the outcome based scoring system, the winner of a fight receives 3 points and the loser receives 0 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. If a fighter misses weight, then the fighter that missed weight will receive 0 points and his opponent will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory.{{cite web|url=http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|title=Rules and Regulations|publisher=PFL|access-date=2018-08-01|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180808170826/http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|archive-date=2018-08-08|url-status=dead}}\n\n===Welterweight===\n{| class=\"wikitable sortable\"\n! States]]"}}}]},"58233392":{"pageid":58233392,"ns":0,"title":"PFL 7 (2018)","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","*":"{{short description|Professional Fighters League MMA event in 2018}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=PFL 7\n|promotion=[[Professional Fighters League]]\n|date= {{start date|2018|08|30}}\n|venue= [[Ocean Resort Casino]]\n|city= [[Atlantic City, New Jersey]]\n|attendance= \n|previousevent=[[PFL 6 (2018 season)|PFL 6]]\n|followingevent=[[PFL 8 (2018 season)|PFL 8]]\n}}\n{{Main|PFL (2018 season)}}\nThe '''''PFL 7''''' [[mixed martial arts]] event for the 2018 season of the [[Professional Fighters League]] was held on August 30, 2018, at the [[Ocean Resort Casino]] in [[Atlantic City, New Jersey]]. It was the seventh and final regular season event of 2018 before the playoffs.{{cite web|url=https://www.pflmma.com/event/pfl-event-7|title=Fight Card - PFL 7|publisher=PFL|access-date=2018-08-28|archive-date=2018-08-28|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180828035928/https://www.pflmma.com/event/pfl-event-7|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Background==\nLightweight's [[Brian Foster (fighter)|Brian Foster]] and [[Ramsey Nijem]] were expected to fight on this card, however both were ruled out of the tournament when Foster was unable to get licensed by the New Jersey athletic commission, and Nijem was unable to gain clearance from a medical suspension. As a result, [[Johnny Case]] vs. [[Jason High]] was made, with the winner determining the 155 divisions final playoff participant.{{cite news|url=https://mmajunkie.com/2018/08/brian-foster-ramsey-nijem-ruled-out-pfl-playoffs-johnny-case-jason-high-winner-in|title=Brian Foster, Ramsey Nijem ruled out of PFL playoffs, Johnny Case vs. Jason High winner gets in|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=MMAjunkie Staff|date=2018-08-27}}\nOn weigh in day however, the substitute fight had its own set back, as High missed the weight limit by 4.2 pounds, and the bout was scratched from the card all together. As a result, Case was awarded 3 points via walkover victory.{{cite news|url=https://mmajunkie.com/2018/08/pfl-7-jason-high-vs-johnny-case-canceled|title=Jason High misses weight for PFL 7 vs. Johnny Case, who qualifies for playoffs after cancellation|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Steven Marrocco|date=2018-08-29}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (NBCSN)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Smealinho Rama]]\n|def.\n|Jamie Abdallah\n|TKO (doctor stoppage)\n|2\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Bazigit Atajev]]\n|def.\n|[[Sean O'Connell (fighter)|Sean O'Connell]]\n|TKO (spinning back kick and punches)\n|1\n|3:30\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Featherweight\n|[[Alexandre Bezerra]]\n|def.\n|Magomed Idrisov\n|KO (flying knee)\n|1\n|3:24\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|Handesson Ferreira\n|def.\n|[[Paul Bradley (fighter)|Paul Bradley]]\n|TKO (punches)\n|1\n|0:20\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|Caio Alencar\n|def.\n|[[Mike Kyle]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (30-27, 30-27, 29-28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent card|Preliminary card (Facebook Watch)|header=no}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Sadibou Sy]]\n|def.\n|[[Caio Magalh\u00e3es]]\n|KO (head kick and punches)\n|1\n|2:06\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Emiliano Sordi]]\n|def.\n|[[Jason Butcher]]\n|KO (punch)\n|1\n|0:16\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Bantamweight\n|[[Umar Nurmagomedov]]\n|def.\n|Saidyokub Kakharamonov\n|Decision (unanimous) (30-27, 30-27, 29-28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Ramazan Kuramagomedov]]\n|def.\n|Robert Hale\n|Decision (unanimous) (30-27, 30-27, 30-27)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|Mo De'Reese\n|def.\n|Leroy Johnson\n|KO (knee)\n|1\n|2:25\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent end|notes=yes}}\n\n==Standings after event==\nThe point system consists of outcome based scoring and bonuses for an early win. Under the outcome based scoring system, the winner of a fight receives 3 points and the loser receives 0 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. If a fighter misses weight, then the fighter that missed weight will receive 0 points and his opponent will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory.{{cite web|url=http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|title=Rules and Regulations|publisher=PFL|access-date=2018-08-23|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180808170826/http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|archive-date=2018-08-08|url-status=dead}}\n\n===Featherweight===\n{{#section:2018 Professional Fighters League season|FTW}}\n\n===Lightweight===\n{{#section:2018 Professional Fighters League season|LW}}\n\n===Welterweight===\n{{#section:2018 Professional Fighters League season|WW}}\n\n===Middleweight===\n{{#section:2018 Professional Fighters League season|MW}}\n\n===Light Heavyweight===\n{{#section:2018 Professional Fighters League season|LHW}}\n\n===Heavyweight===\n{{#section:2018 Professional Fighters League season|HW}}\n\n\u265b = Clinched playoff spot ---\n\ud83d\udeab = Ruled out ---\nE = Eliminated\n\n''NOTE: Prior to [[PFL 8 (2018 season)|PFL 8]], which started the PFL playoff, [[Valdrin Istrefi]] was ruled out because of an injury. Professional Fighters League|2026]]|title=Seasons}}\n\nThe '''Professional Fighters League''' ('''PFL''') is an American [[mixed martial arts]] league founded by venture capitalist [[Donn Davis]] in 2017 and launched in 2018,{{Cite web |last=Goldsmith |first=Jill |date=2022-01-25 |title=MMA's Professional Fighters League Extends ESPN Deal Starting With New Season |url=https://deadline.com/2022/01/professional-fighters-league-pfl-espn-deal-mma-mixed-martial-arts-1234918688/ |access-date=2022-11-20 |website=Deadline |language=en-US}}{{Cite web |last=Connolly |first=Matt |title=MMA In 2017 Part II: Under New Ownership, WSOF Prepares To Rebrand As Pro Fighters League (Updated) |url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/mattconnolly/2017/04/19/mma-in-2017-part-ii-wsof-excited-for-new-innovations-new-fighters-under-new-ownership/ |access-date=2022-11-20 |website=Forbes |language=en}} following the acquisition and restructuring of the former World Series of Fighting (WSOF) promotion in 2017 by MMAX Investment Partners.{{Cite news|last=Mindenhall|first=Chuck|title=PFL has come a long way in a short time \u2013 so far that WSOF feels like a distant memory|url=https://www.nytimes.com/athletic/1071084/2019/07/10/pfl-has-come-a-long-way-in-a-short-time-so-far-that-wsof-feels-like-a-distant-memory/|access-date=2020-11-05|website=The Athletic}}{{Cite web|last=Neibauer|first=Michael|title=Russ Ramsey, Donn Davis launch professional MMA league with $10M prize pool|url=https://www.bizjournals.com/washington/news/2017/04/19/russ-ramsey-donn-davis-launch-professional-mma.html|access-date=2021-11-03|website=www.bizjournals.com}} It is the first major MMA organization in which individual athletes compete in a regular season, post-season and championship, rather than on a year-round basis.{{Cite news|url=https://deadline.com/2018/03/professional-fighters-league-mgm-television-group-mark-burnett-1202355236/|title=Professional Fighters League To Partner With MGM Television Group For New Programming|last=Ramos|first=Dino-Ray|date=2018-03-29|work=Deadline|access-date=2018-06-19|language=en-US}} They have since transitioned from the league format back into a year-round basis.\n\nThe PFL currently puts on fights across eight weight-divisions: women's flyweight, [[Bantamweight (MMA)|bantamweight]], [[Featherweight (MMA)|featherweight]], women's Featherweight, [[Lightweight (MMA)|lightweight]], [[Welterweight (MMA)|welterweight]], [[Light heavyweight (MMA)|light heavyweight]] and [[Heavyweight (MMA)|heavyweight]].{{Cite web|date=May 11, 2021|first=Akaash|last=Sharma|title=Everything You Need To Know About Professional Fighters League|url=https://www.one37pm.com/strength/sports/professional-fighters-league|access-date=2021-10-27|website=www.one37pm.com}}{{Cite web|last=Mitra|first=Anurag|title=Professional Fighters League: All you need to know about the unique MMA league|url=https://www.sportskeeda.com/mma/news-professional-fighters-league-pfl-all-need-know-unique-mma-league|access-date=2021-10-27|website=www.sportskeeda.com|date=24 December 2020 |language=en-us}} The PFL's matches are held inside a 10-sided [[Mixed martial arts#Fighting area|mixed martial arts cage]] known as the SmartCage, and adhere to the [[Mixed martial arts rules|unified rules of mixed martial arts]]. The PFL initially prohibited all [[Elbow (strike)|elbow strikes]],{{Cite web|last=Albano|first=Thomas|date=2021-04-19|title=What are the rules for the Professional Fighters League?|url=https://fansided.com/2021/04/19/rules-professional-fighters-league/|access-date=2021-10-29|website=FanSided|language=en-US}}{{Cite web|last=Dachman|first=Jason|title=Inside the 'SmartCage': Data Sits at the Center of the Professional Fighters League Universe|url=https://www.sportsvideo.org/2021/05/24/inside-the-smartcage-data-sits-at-the-at-the-center-of-the-pro-fighters-league-universe/|access-date=2021-10-29|website=Sports Video Group|date=24 May 2021 |language=en}}{{Cite web |last=Professional Fighters League |date=March 3, 2023 |title=Professional Fighters League Official Rules |url=https://pflmma-prod.s3.amazonaws.com/settings/league_attachments/5b1d7d77cfe8189c56ad88bbb1e24a1b-1.pdf |access-date=June 26, 2023 |website=Professional Fighters League}} but in November 2024, it was announced that they would finally be allowed, starting with the 2024 Championship season.{{Cite web|last=Dewar|first=Val|date=2024-11-26|title=PFL to finally allow elbows in championship bouts|url=https://www.sportingnews.com/us/mma/news/pfl-finally-allow-elbows-championship-bouts/468465ffc52142fd2361b8bb#:~:text=The%20Tuesday%20morning%20of%20their,and%20in%20the%20foreseeable%20future.|access-date=2024-11-26|website=SportingNews|language=en-US}}\n\nThe PFL's inaugural event took place on June 7, 2018, at the [[Hulu Theater]] at [[Madison Square Garden]] in [[New York City]]. At the end, each champion of the six weight classes won a championship prize of $1 million each.{{Cite news|url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/brianmazique/2018/04/16/how-the-pfl-plans-to-change-the-way-fans-fighters-and-sponsors-see-the-sport-of-mma/)|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180811065708/https://www.forbes.com/sites/brianmazique/2018/04/16/how-the-pfl-plans-to-change-the-way-fans-fighters-and-sponsors-see-the-sport-of-mma/)|url-status=dead|archive-date=August 11, 2018|title=How The PFL Plans To Change The Way Fans, Fighters And Sponsors See MMA|last=Mazique|first=Brian|work=Forbes|access-date=2018-06-19|language=en}}{{Cite web|date=2018-06-06|title=PFL 1 debut card official in New York after heavyweight musical chairs|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2018/06/pfl-1-debut-card-official-in-new-york-after-heavyweight-musical-chairs|access-date=2021-10-27|website=MMA Junkie|language=en-US}}\n\nAs of 2026, the PFL no longer features a league format. Now, there are multiple championship belts that are contested in several different weight classes.\n\n== History ==\n\n=== Founding and WSOF acquisition ===\n[[File:PFL 2018 Photo.jpg|thumb|right|alt=Professional Fighters League event in 2018.|Professional Fighters League event in 2018]]\nThe World Series of Fighting (WSOF) was formed in 2012, after signing a broadcast deal with the [[NBC Sports Network]]. Soon after WSOF's first event was announced, it would be confirmed that [[Bas Rutten]] and [[Michael Schiavello]] would be commentators.{{Cite news|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/09/tna-contract-nixes-kurt-angle-commentator-job-at-world-series-of-fighting-1|archive-url=https://archive.today/20130129032326/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/09/tna-contract-nixes-kurt-angle-commentator-job-at-world-series-of-fighting-1|url-status=dead|archive-date=2013-01-29|title=TNA contract nixes Kurt Angle commentator job at World Series of Figh\u2026|date=2013-01-29|work=archive.is|access-date=2018-06-19}} \nJazz Securo was the official voice and Ring Announcer of WSOF.{{Cite web|url=https://www.imdb.com/name/nm5874158/|title=Jazz Securo|website=IMDb|access-date=2017-01-18}}\nThe WSOF held over 35 events in multiple countries including the United States, Canada, [[Japan]], [[China]] and the [[Philippines]],{{Cite news|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2017/4/19/15355554/wsof-becomes-professional-fighters-league-inaugural-season-set-to-kick-off-next-year|title=WSOF becomes Professional Fighters League; Inaugural season set to kick off next year|work=MMA Fighting|access-date=2018-06-19}} and was best known for featuring fighters such as [[Justin Gaethje]], [[Marlon Moraes]] and [[Lance Palmer]].{{Cite web|date=2016-06-23|title=WSOF to hold first tourney in the Philippines|url=https://www.manilatimes.net/2016/06/23/sports/wsof-to-hold-first-tourney-in-the-philippines/269515|access-date=2021-10-27|website=The Manila Times|language=en}}\n\nIn 2017, MMAX Investment Partners, a group of [[McLean, Virginia]] investors, which included [[Investor|financier]] [[Russ Ramsey]], Mark Leschly, [[Businessperson|businessman]] Ted Leonsis, [[Washington Nationals]]' owner [[Mark Lerner]] and future [[Virginia]] [[Governor (United States)|governor]] [[Glenn Youngkin]], acquired the assets of the WSOF.[https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1694675/000169467517000001/xslFormDX01/primary_doc.xml SEC Form D - MMAX Investment Partners, Inc]{{Cite web|title=MMAX Investment Partners, Inc. SEC Registration|url=https://sec.report/CIK/0001694675|access-date=2022-01-04|website=sec.report}}{{Cite web |last=Okamoto |first=Brett |date=2017-04-19 |title=Professional Fighters League to debut in 2018 |url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/19193269/world-series-fighting-relaunched-professional-fighters-league |access-date=2022-07-08 |website=ESPN.com |language=en}}{{Cite web |last=Badenhausen |first=Kurt |title=The New Fight Game: How An MMA Startup Wants To Capture The Sport's 450 Million Global Fans |url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/kurtbadenhausen/2018/12/28/the-new-fight-game-how-an-mma-startup-wants-to-capture-the-sports-450-million-global-fans/ |access-date=2022-11-20 |website=Forbes |language=en}} It was announced that the WSOF would be restructured as the Professional Fighters League (PFL), with owner Davis as founder and with the inaugural season taking place in 2018.{{Cite web|last=Helwani|first=Ariel|date=2017-04-19|title=WSOF becomes Professional Fighters League; Inaugural season set to kick off next year|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2017/4/19/15355554/wsof-becomes-professional-fighters-league-inaugural-season-set-to-kick-off-next-year|access-date=2021-10-27|website=MMA Fighting|language=en}} After the restructuring, WSOF CEO Carlos Silva and WSOF president and co-founder [[Ray Sefo]] joined PFL, with Silva as president of PFL and Sefo as president of fight operations. Sefo was previously an MMA fighter and a six-time [[Muay Thai]] [[IFMA World Muaythai Championships|world champion]].{{Cite web |last=Cochrane |first=Joe |title=PFL President Ray Sefo: 'We're Doing the Right Things' |url=https://combatpress.com/2021/08/pfl-president-ray-sefo-were-doing-the-right-things/ |access-date=2022-07-08 |website=Combat Press |language=en-US}}{{Cite web |last=Perez |first=Juan |date=2021-02-24 |title=Meet The Legendary Fighter Who Helped Build PFL's Star-Studded Roster |url=https://www.ibtimes.com/meet-legendary-fighter-who-helped-build-pfls-star-studded-roster-3151184 |access-date=2022-07-08 |website=International Business Times}} In January 2018, former [[National Football League]] executive [[Peter Murray (American businessman)|Peter Murray]] was appointed CEO of the league. That year, in its inaugural season as the PFL, the promotion introduced a [[Sports league|league model]], the first MMA promotion to do so.{{Cite web |last=Davies |first=Harry |date=2022-05-13 |title=PFL boss hails league format for bringing \"compelling product\" to MMA |url=https://www.mirror.co.uk/sport/other-sports/mma/pfl-boss-hails-league-format-26941876 |access-date=2022-11-21 |website=mirror |language=en}}{{Cite web |last=Kaplan |first=Daniel |title=Peter Murray |url=https://www.sportsbusinessjournal.com/Journal/Issues/2006/03/20/Forty-Under-40/Peter-Murray.aspx |access-date=2022-11-21 |website=www.sportsbusinessjournal.com |date=20 March 2006 |language=en}}\n\n===2020\u20132021===\n[[File:MMAWorldSeriesOfFightingLogo.jpg|thumb|The former WSOF/PFL logo (2012-2018)]]Following a partnership with [[blockchain]] reward app Socios.com in October 2020, PFL launched a fan [[non-fungible token]] (NFT) and became the first sports league to launch such a token.{{Cite web|title=PrI Token|url=https://www.sporttechie.com/professional-fighters-league-launches-socios-fan-token|access-date=2021-10-27|website=www.sporttechie.com|date=April 2021 |language=en}}\nIn February 2021, PFL announced a $65 million financing round which expanded the total funding to $175 million. Super Fight participants will earn at least 50 percent of the pay-per-view revenue, significantly higher than the 20 percent earned by fighters in the UFC.{{Cite news |last=Rhim |first=Kris |date=2023-01-05 |title=Jake Paul to Fight in Mixed Martial Arts in Deal With P.F.L. |language=en-US |work=The New York Times |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2023/01/05/sports/jake-paul-mma-boxing.html |access-date=2023-01-05 |issn=0362-4331}}\n\nAfter ten PFL athletes \u2013 including former champions \u2013 failed their drug tests during the first half of season 2023, the organization decided to hire [[United States Anti-Doping Agency|USADA]] to oversee and test their athletes for banned substances, much akin to what their rival [[UFC]] has already implemented.{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/37996093/pfl-hires-usada-run-anti-doping-program|title=PFL hires USADA to run its anti-doping program|date=July 11, 2023|work=[[ESPN]]|author=Marc Raimondi}}\n\nOn August 30, 2023, the promotion announced that it had sold a minority stake for an initial investment of $100 million to SRJ Sports Investments \u2013 a branch of [[Public Investment Fund]]. July 2026, it was announced that PFL would merge with the [[Most Valuable Promotions]] organization. The new company will be backed by existing PFL investors Founding Investors 885 Capital and Knighthead Capital Management while being led by [[Jake Paul]] and [[Nakisa Bidarian]] with PFL executive John Martin still as CEO. The combined company will operate under the MVP banner.{{Cite web|date=2026-07-30|title=Most Valuable Promotions and Professional Fighters League Merge to Create a New Global Combat Sports Platform Powerhouse|url=https://pflmma.com/news/most-valuable-promotions-and-professional-fighters-league-merge-to-create-a-new-global-combat-sports-platform-powerhouse|access-date=2026-08-06|website=PFLMMA.com|language=en-US}}\n\n== PFL Challenger Series ==\n{{See also|PFL Challenger Series}}\n'''[[PFL Challenger Series]]''' was an American [[mixed martial arts]] series. Young and up-and-coming male and female MMA fighters will compete for a slot in the PFL tournament season and a chance at $1 million. Each week, the PFL Challenger Series will consist of a celebrity guest panel featuring personalities in film, athletics and sports. In the 2021 debut airing of the PFL Challenger Series, the celebrity guest panel included NFL stars [[Ray Lewis]] and [[Todd Gurley]] and entertainers [[Jeremy Piven]] and [[Wiz Khalifa]].{{Cite Former [[List_of_UFC_champions#Heavyweight_Championship|UFC Heavyweight Champion]] [[Francis Ngannou]] acts as the chairman of the league that is set to launch in the second quarter of 2025.{{cite web|url=https://cagesidepress.com/2024/07/10/with-francis-ngannou-as-chairman-pfl-africa-set-for-q2-2025-launch/|title=With Francis Ngannou as Chairman, PFL Africa Set for Q2 2025 Launch|date=July 10, 2024|publisher=cagesidepress.com}}\n\n== Roster ==\n{{main|List of current PFL fighters}}\n\nFor each season, the PFL roster begins with 72 fighters in six [[weight class]]es: five men's weight classes and one women's weight class. PFL additionally signs fighters to development contracts from the PFL Challenger Series. These fighters occasionally appear as alternates in the league season or as showcase fights opening the league events.\n\nFor the 2018 season, the PFL roster included 72 fighters in six weight classes.{{Cite news|url=https://mmajunkie.com/2018/04/professional-fighters-league-exclusive-pfl-roster-reveal-wsof-millionaire-tournament-mma/2|title=PFL roster revealed: Six of these 72 fighters The top eight in each weight-class faced off in bracket-style, single elimination playoff fights on Saturday nights in October. The PFL 2018 season concluded on December 31, 2018, with six championship bouts back-to-back with a $10 million prize pool.{{Cite news|url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/brianmazique/2018/04/16/professional-fighters-league-pfl-mma-format-roster-schedule-and-free-tv-model-revealed/)|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190826225941/https://www.forbes.com/sites/brianmazique/2018/04/16/professional-fighters-league-pfl-mma-format-roster-schedule-and-free-tv-model-revealed/)|url-status=dead|archive-date=August 26, 2019|title=Professional Fighters League's MMA Format, Roster, Schedule And Free-TV Model Revealed|last=Mazique|first=Brian|work=Forbes|access-date=2018-06-19|language=en}} The first season was headlined by a fight between Andre Harrison about [[Jumabieke Tuerxun]] and featured bouts in the featherweight and heavyweight divisions.{{Cite web|date=2018-06-06|title=PFL 1 debut card official in New York after heavyweight musical chairs|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2018/06/pfl-1-debut-card-official-in-new-york-after-heavyweight-musical-chairs|access-date=2021-10-29|website=MMA Junkie|language=en-US}}{{Cite web|last=Knapp|first=Brian|title=Jumabieke Tuerxun No Match for Undefeated Andre Harrison in PFL 1 Main Event|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Jumabieke-Tuerxun-No-Match-for-Undefeated-Andre-Harrison-in-PFL-1-Main-Event-137869|access-date=2021-10-29|website=Sherdog}}\n\nIn 2018, the seven regular-season events were held at the Hulu Theater at Madison Square Garden, the Chicago Theatre, GWU Smith Center, Nassau Coliseum and Ocean Resort Casino.{{Cite news|url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/brianmazique/2018/04/16/professional-fighters-league-pfl-mma-format-roster-schedule-and-free-tv-model-revealed/#4da63622c401)|title=Professional Fighters League's MMA Format, Roster, Schedule And Free-TV Model Revealed|last=Mazique|first=Brian|work=Forbes|access-date=2018-06-19|language=en}} The playoff events was held at the Ernest N. Morial Convention Center, the Long Beach Arena, and the St. Elizabeths East Entertainment and Sports Arena. The championship event was held at the Hulu Theater at Madison Square Garden.{{Cite news|url=https://www.mmamania.com/2018/4/16/17240098/pfl-roster-event-schedule-released-2018-championship-season|title=PFL roster, event schedule released for 2018 championship season|work=MMAmania.com|access-date=2018-06-19}}\n\n====2019====\n{{Main|2019 Professional Fighters League season}}\n\nThe PFL 2019 regular season consisted of six weight classes and six events spanning from May to August. Playoffs consisted of three events and the season culminates with the Championship Event on New Year's Eve 2019.\n\n====2020====\nThe PFL 2020 season was postponed due to the [[COVID-19 pandemic]].{{Cite web|last=Sarkar|first=Raj|date=2020-09-28|title=EXCLUSIVE: PFL CEO Peter Murray Opens up About 2021 Season and More|url=http://www.essentiallysports.com/mma-news-exclusive-pfl-ceo-peter-murray-opens-up-about-2021-season-and-more/|access-date=2020-10-15|website=EssentiallySports|language=en-US}} As a measure to relieve the financial pressure of the athletes, the organization decided to hand out monthly [[stipend]]s to the contracted fighters.{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/05/ray-sefo-talks-difficulty-in-decision-to-cancel-pfls-2020-season|title=Ray Sefo talks difficulty in decision to cancel PFL's 2020 season|author=Farah Hannoun|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=May 13, 2020}} Reactions to the stipend were mixed, with fighter [[Lance Palmer]] complaining about the amount of the stipend and with fighter [[Natan Schulte]] applauding the monthly stipend. According to multiple fighters, the stipend was $1,000 per month.{{Cite web|last=Martin|first=Damon|date=2020-07-28|title=Two-time champ Lance Palmer believes relationship with PFL is beyond repair, asks for release|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2020/7/28/21344730/two-time-champ-lance-palmer-believes-relationship-with-pfl-is-beyond-repair-asks-for-release|access-date=2020-07-29|website=MMA Fighting|language=en}}{{Cite web|last=Cruz|first=Guilherme|date=2020-04-26|title=Two-time PFL champ Natan Schulte celebrates monthly stipend for fighters during coronavirus outbreak|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2020/4/26/21228535/pfl-champion-natan-schulte-monthly-stipend-fighters-coronavirus|access-date=2020-10-15|website=MMA Fighting|language=en}}\n\nIn September 2020, PFL introduced a new series called \"Inside the Season,\" produced in partnership with the [[United States Marine Corp]], that detailed the journey fighters go on through training, regular season, playoffs and championships. The series is narrated by actor [[John C. McGinley]] and airs on [[ESPNews]].{{Cite web|last=Becker|first=Dana|date=2020-09-15|title=PFL To Introduce Special Inside The Season Series By The Marines|url=https://www.themix.net/2020/09/pfl-to-introduce-special-inside-the-season-series-by-the-marines/|access-date=2022-01-21|website=TheMix.net|language=en}}\n\n====2021====\n{{Main|2021 in Professional Fighters League}}\n\nTwo-time Olympic gold medalist and top pound-for-pound women's boxer [[Claressa Shields]] signed a three-year contract with the Professional Fighters League to compete in the Women's Lightweight division and former UFC lightweight champ [[Anthony Pettis]] who joined the Professional Fighters League ahead of the 2021 regular season.{{Cite web|date=2021-01-27|title=Claressa Shields, world's greatest female boxer, is coming for the MMA crown|url=https://nypost.com/2021/01/27/claressa-shields-worlds-greatest-female-boxer-is-coming-for-the-mma-crown/|access-date=2021-10-27|website=New York Post|language=en-US}}{{Cite web|title=Claressa Shields signs with PFL as women's boxing champion makes transition to MMA|url=https://www.cbssports.com/boxing/news/claressa-shields-signs-with-pfl-as-womens-boxing-champion-makes-transition-to-mma/|access-date=2021-10-27|website=CBSSports.com|date=December 2020 |language=en}}{{Cite web|last=Vreeland|first=Daniel|date=2021-01-28|title=Anthony Pettis: \"Showtime\" Shares What Led Him to the PFL|url=https://cagesidepress.com/2021/01/28/anthony-pettis-showtime-shares-led-pfl/|access-date=2021-10-27|website=Cageside Press|language=en-US}}\n\nPFL's 2021 roster included 60 fighters competing across six weight classes. The late additions to the season were the welterweights [[Gleison Tibau]], [[Alexey Kunchenko]] and Jason Ponet, featherweights [[Chris Wade (fighter)|Chris Wade]] and Anthony Dizy, women's lightweights [[Kaitlin Young]] and Taylor Guardado and light heavyweight Nick Roehrick.{{cite web |title=PFL finalizes 2021 roster with additional signings including UFC vet Gleison Tibau, Invicta FC's Kaitlin Young |url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2021/2/25/22301428/pfl-finalizes-2021-roster-signings-including-ufc-vet-gleison-tibau-and-invicta-fcs-kaitlin-young |website=mmafighting.com |date=25 February 2021 |access-date=25 February 2021}}\n\nIn December 2021, PFL announced a multi-year partnership for its new PFL Challenger Series with [[streaming television]] provider [[fuboTV]] on its Fubo Sports Network.{{Cite web|last=Gladen|first=Sam|date=2021-03-18|title=fuboTV, Professional Fighters League team up for special programing|url=https://fansided.com/2021/03/18/fubotv-professional-fighters-league-team-special-programing/|access-date=2022-01-21|website=FanSided|language=en-US}} Set to debut in February 2022, the new event will see fighters competing for a chance to earn a PFL contract and will incorporate a celebrity judging panel, a fan vote, sports betting and gamification.{{Cite web|last=Bucholtz|first=Andrew|date=2021-03-18|title=PFL Vault is moving to Fridays on fubo Sports Network|url=https://awfulannouncing.com/mma/pfl-vault-fubo-sports-network-fridays.html|access-date=2022-01-21|website=Awful Announcing|language=en-US}}\n\n====2022 - 2026====\n{{Main|2022 Professional Fighters League season}}\n{{Main|2023 Professional Fighters League season}}\n{{Main|2024 in Professional Fighters League}}\n{{Main|2025 in Professional Fighters League}}\n{{Main|2026 in Professional Fighters League}}\n\n==Broadcast==\nOn January 29, 2018, the League announced it had reached a multi-platform distribution deal for the inaugural 2018 season with [[NBC Sports Group]] and [[Facebook]].{{Cite news|url=https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20180129005882/en/Professional-Fighters-League-Reaches-Exclusive-Multi-Platform-Distribution|title=Professional Fighters League Reaches Exclusive Multi-Platform Distribution Agreements with NBC Sports Group and Facebook|access-date=2018-06-19|language=en}} Within the United States, NBC Sports Group established a live Thursday night PFL franchise, presenting seven regular-season live events in prime time exclusively on [[NBCSN]] beginning June 7, 2018, through the end of August 2018. Events televised by NBCSN also streamed on [[NBCSports.com]] and the [[NBC Sports]] app. All regular season events had encore telecasts on NBCSN later the same night. Outside the U.S., [[Facebook]] streamed up to six hours of live coverage for each event. Facebook also opened each event with a live 30-minute pre-fight show and closed each with a live 30-minute post-fight show. In the U.S., fans were able to watch the first three hours of undercard coverage of each event on Facebook prior to NBCSN's presentation of the main bouts.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2018/01/pfl-2018-broadcast-deal-events-nbc-sports-group-facebook|title=PFL announces 2018 events and broadcast deal with NBC Sports Group and Facebook|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=January 29, 2018}}\n\nBeginning in February 2019, the PFL's events are broadcast by [[ESPN]] in the United States and [[The Sports Network|TSN]] in Canada.{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.com/2019/02/pfl-espn-multiyear-broadcast-deal-second-season-tournament-kayla-harrison|title=PFL signs broadcast deal with ESPN for second season|date=February 25, 2019|publisher=mmajunkie.com}} PFL events and playoff matches air on [[ESPN+]] and [[ESPN2]], with the Championship Event on New Year's Eve airing exclusively on ESPN2 and [[ESPN Deportes]].{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/2/25/18240205/pfl-signs-multi-year-deal-with-espn|title=PFL signs multi-year deal with ESPN|author=Alexander K. Lee|publisher=mmafighting.com|date=February 25, 2019}}{{cite web |last1=Levy |first1=Joe |title='With OTT there is no better time to be doing this': Why the PFL is betting big on DTC |url=https://www.sportspro.com/insights/interviews/professional-fighters-league-ott-streaming-espn-peter-murray-mma-olympics/ |website=SportsPro |date=28 May 2019 |access-date=7 June 2019 |language=en}}\n\nThe PFL international distribution is handled by combat sports rights distributor Fighting Spirit.{{cite web|url=https://www.pflmma.com/news/pfl-teams-with-fighting-spirit-to-expand-global-reach|title=Professional Fighters League teams with Fighting Spirit to expand global reach|author=PFL|publisher=pflmma.com|date=March 4, 2019}} PFL airs on [[Claro Sports]] in Hispanic America, [[Combate Global|Combate]] in Brazil, [[MMA TV]] in the United Kingdom, [https://www.mmatv.co.uk/pfl/ MMA TV] in Nordic countries, [[Match TV]] and Boets TV in Russia, [[JSBC]] in China, [[beIN Sports]] in the Middle East and Northern Africa, and [[ESPN Africa|ESPN]] in sub-Saharan Africa.\n\nIn August 2020, the PFL launched an over the top (OTT) platform via its mobile app. In October 2021, the PFL signed a distribution deal with sports media company Wave.tv.{{Cite web|date=2020-10-14|title=PFL Looks to Digital Content Expansion to Bring in More Fans|url=https://frontofficesports.com/pfl-wavetv-partnership/|access-date=2021-10-27|website=Front Office Sports|language=en-US}}{{Cite web|title=WAVE.tv Secures Content Deal With PFL|url=https://www.sporttechie.com/wavetv-secures-content-deal-with-pfl|access-date=2021-10-27|website=www.sporttechie.com|date=25 September 2020 |language=en}}\n\nAs of 2026, the PFL is broadcast in 160 countries, through media partners like [[ESPN]] in the United States, [[TV Globo|Globo]] in Brazil, which renewed its rights as the exclusive broadcast partner for the country in a multi-year agreement in March 2024, [[Bild (German TV channel)|Bild]] in Germany, RMC Sports in France, [[Eurosport (Indian TV channel)|Eurosport]] in India, and Telesport in Russia.{{Cite web|date=2021-04-22|title=PFL Continues Global Expansion With New Broadcast Partners|url=https://www.themix.net/2021/04/pfl-continues-global-expansion-with-new-broadcast-partners/|access-date=2021-10-27|website=TheMix.net|language=en}}{{cite web |last1=Bresaola |date=5 March 2024}} In January 2022, it was announced that PFL and ESPN struck a multi-year contract extension for broadcasting rights in the United States.{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/33143857 |title=Professional Fighters League expects to keep champ Kayla Harrison|date=January 25, 2022|access-date=2022-05-11|work=[[ESPN]]}}\n\nIn October 2022, the PFL is broadcast by Fast Sports HD in Pakistan{{citation needed|date=November 2022}}\n\nIn February 2024, PFL announced its agreement with [[TV Nova (Czech Republic)|TV Nova]] to become the exclusive broadcast partner for [[Czech Republic]] and [[Slovakia]] for 2024-2025, followed by an agreement with Sub-Saharan African organization [[SuperSport (South African broadcaster)|SuperSport]] in March 2024 to broadcast all PFL programming in the region.{{cite web |last1=Cunningham |first1=Euan |title=PFL in Nova tie-up across Czech Republic and Slovakia |url=https://www.sportcal.com/media/pfl-in-nova-tie-up-across-czech-republic-and-slovakia/ |website=Sportcal |access-date=6 May 2024 |date=22 February 2024}}{{cite web |last1=Saleh |first1=Tariq 2024 |date=11 March 2024}} In March 2024, PFL announced its agreement with [[Warner Bros. Discovery]] for exclusive rights to carry the Bellator Champions Series for the United States. The 8 events will stream live on [[Max (streaming service)|Max]], which began with Bellator Champions Series Belfast on March 22.{{cite web |last1=Mazique |first1=Brian |title=PFL Announces New Deal With Warner Bros Discovery For Bellator Content |url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/brianmazique/2024/03/19/pfl-announces-new-deal-with-warner-bros-discovery-for-bellator-content/?sh=7bffc7e1dbdc |website=Forbes |access-date=27 May 2024 |date=19 March 2024}}\n\n=== Broadcast team ===\nPFL's 2018 broadcast team for live events and pre- and post-event shows included [[Randy Couture]], [[Bas Rutten]], [[Yves Edwards]], [[Caroline Pearce]] and [[Todd Harris]]. Harris provided play-by-play while Couture provided color commentary. Rutten and Pearce conducted pre- and post-fight interviews and Edwards offered video breakdowns and fight analysis.{{Cite
